**Position.** The speaker addresses someone she cannot know and who will not receive the address.

**Surface.** A second-person letter to an unidentified person, with no delivery possible, using the full intimacy of direct address.

**Fold.** The second person gives the stranger a presence the facts deny him, and the poem's attention becomes a possession the speaker chooses: she, not he, now decides what he is.

**Active site.** The first sentence addressed to him.

**Forbidden.** Naming him. A delivery. A reply.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know the addressee or reach him.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the addressee has power over the speaker's memory. Addressed to a stranger with no hold on her, the letter is a conceit.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-as-02, cf-as-04. Cancels with cf-he-02, which sets the same unreachable addressee; one trial takes one.

**Engines admitted.** Body-cost rule.

**Device cluster.** A (addressee position, apostrophe).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-as-04 fading description

```text
handle: Assailant | axis: sideways | imposed by: memory that will not keep a stranger distinct
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker's only knowledge of her subject is a description that is already fading.

**Surface.** Late in the poem the speaker resolves to remember better, lists the few physical facts she holds, and admits she fears confusing him with others.

**Fold.** The fear of forgetting reverses the expected fear. She dreads losing the one person she might be expected to want to lose, because the description is her only hold on what happened.

**Active site.** The resolution to remember.

**Forbidden.** New facts about him. Relief. A stated reason why remembering matters.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot keep him distinct, and cannot know whether she already confuses him with others.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It holds where the memory is the speaker's only evidence. Elsewhere it reads as whimsy.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-as-02, cf-as-03, cf-me-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (list syntax), E (closure), G (witness relation).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-wh-02 double name

```text
handle: White Lady | axis: upward | imposed by: a name that belongs to two powers at once
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The power the speakers face carries a name that fits two powers, one present and one historical, and the speakers cannot separate them.

**Surface.** One name used throughout for both. A gloss that fixes one meaning, and images that belong to the other.

**Fold.** The name makes the present harm legible through the historical one, so the reader cannot read either without the other.

**Active site.** The first image that belongs to the historical power rather than the glossed one.

**Forbidden.** Separating the two. Explaining the second meaning. A third.

**Knowledge test.** The speakers cannot say which power they face, because to them it is one.

**Stake.** High. It depends on a real historical continuity between the two powers. Without one, it is a pun.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-wh-03, cf-wh-04.

**Engines admitted.** Anti-laundering engine default.

**Device cluster.** D (personification, allusion), G (appropriation risk).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-wh-03 overheard seduction

```text
handle: White Lady | axis: upward | imposed by: a power whose speech is addressed to others
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speakers hear the power speak, and it speaks to their children, not to them.

**Surface.** The power's voice quoted in its own idiom of intimacy, addressed to the young, inside the speakers' poem.

**Fold.** Quoting the seduction lets the reader hear what the children hear and what the parents cannot answer, since none of it is addressed to them.

**Active site.** The quoted seduction.

**Forbidden.** The parents answering the seduction directly. The children replying. Irony in the quotation.

**Knowledge test.** The speakers can hear the offer and cannot enter the exchange.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It holds where the seduction is effective on the page. Made ridiculous, it tells the reader the danger is small.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-wh-02, cf-wh-04.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface, where the seduction's tenderness carries the threat.

**Device cluster.** A (addressee position), D (prosopopoeia, quotation).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-wh-04 ransom question

```text
handle: White Lady | axis: upward | imposed by: a captor that sets no price
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The only speech the community has toward the power is to ask the price of return.

**Surface.** Repeated questions of cost, payment, and debt addressed to the power at the close, unanswered.

**Fold.** Asking the price concedes that the children are property. The question repeats the captor's logic and exposes it, and the community cannot stop asking.

**Active site.** The last question.

**Forbidden.** An answer. A price. Defiance that abandons the question.

**Knowledge test.** The speakers cannot know the price, because the power sets none.

**Stake.** High. It requires a real captor with a real hold. Otherwise it reads as rhetoric.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-wh-02, cf-wh-03, cf-hu-02, cf-mc-02. Cancels with cf-he-03: both must close on an unanswered question, and a trial closes once.

**Engines admitted.** Anti-laundering engine default.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation, parallelism), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-no-02 unglossed destination

```text
handle: Norway | axis: outward | imposed by: a destination known only by name
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The characters head toward, or fail to head toward, a place they know only by name, and the poem never says what it stands for.

**Surface.** A real place-name repeated as goal and as reason, with the story kept literal throughout and no gloss.

**Fold.** Keeping the name literal lets it carry every departure the reader can think of, and keeps the poem from committing to one.

**Active site.** The first time the name is used as a reason rather than a place.

**Forbidden.** A gloss. A description of the place. A second destination.

**Knowledge test.** No one in the poem knows the place, and the reader cannot know which meaning the poem holds.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs a name with real pull on the characters, such as a family's origin. An arbitrary place-name reads as whimsy.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-no-03, cf-no-04. Cancels with cf-ap-04: both hold readings level, and the second adds nothing.

**Engines admitted.** Load-bearing foreignness.

**Device cluster.** D (symbol), B (repetition).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-no-03 forgotten goal

```text
handle: Norway | axis: outward | imposed by: age that erodes a lifelong want
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The ones who wanted the place forget what it was before they ever reach it.

**Surface.** A late question from those who longed, asking where the place is, set against their earlier certainty.

**Fold.** The want outlives its object. The reader watches desire come loose from knowledge, which makes the longing purer and the loss complete.

**Active site.** The question.

**Forbidden.** Remembering again. The forgetting explained clinically.

**Knowledge test.** They cannot know what they wanted, and the one who remembers cannot give it back to them.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs real aging on the page.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-no-02, cf-no-04.

**Engines admitted.** Body-cost rule, since the aging is carried in the body.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation), A (temporal deixis).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-no-04 reversed departure

```text
handle: Norway | axis: outward | imposed by: a departure the one who urged it must make himself
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The one who urged others toward a place goes there himself, first, and is parted from them by going.

**Surface.** Late in the poem the urger announces his own departure in the words used earlier, and the close shows the separation from the far side.

**Fold.** The reversal turns the urging into a rehearsal of the urger's own going, and the reader rereads the whole poem as a leave-taking.

**Active site.** The announcement in borrowed words.

**Forbidden.** A reason given for going. A return. The others following.

**Knowledge test.** The urger cannot know the place except by going, and cannot report back.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs the others to have wanted the place for real.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-no-02, cf-no-03. Cancels with cf-hl-03: one takes the departure, and the other must never begin it.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (turn), A (pronoun shift).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ul-02 exhaustive image

```text
handle: Ultrasound | axis: outward | imposed by: an image that shows every part and not the whole
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker has a complete image of a body and cannot find in it the thing that makes it a person.

**Surface.** An inventory of visible parts, anatomical and exact, followed by the one thing the image does not show, named as missing.

**Fold.** The list's completeness makes the absence exact. The missing thing is located by everything around it.

**Active site.** The naming of what is missing.

**Forbidden.** The missing thing found. A vague inventory. Mysticism.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot see the missing thing, and the image cannot show it.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds when the missing thing matters to the speaker more than everything shown.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ul-03. Cancels with cf-mt-03: both locate a limit by the reach of the means of knowing.

**Engines admitted.** Defamiliarization engine.

**Device cluster.** A (zoom by noun specificity), C (list syntax).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ul-03 recognition by inheritance

```text
handle: Ultrasound | axis: outward | imposed by: a bond that can be proved only by resemblance
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker expects to be recognized by someone who cannot yet know him, and the only proof of the bond will be a trait he passed on.

**Surface.** A closing prediction that the other will know him by a shared physical feature, delivered as a joke.

**Fold.** Moving the proof of the bond from spirit to body lets the poem end on certainty without claiming the thing it could not see.

**Active site.** The final prediction.

**Forbidden.** A spiritual recognition. Solemnity. Certainty about anything else.

**Knowledge test.** The other cannot know him until birth, and he cannot know the other except by a resemblance still to come.

**Stake.** Low to moderate. Portable to any address between parent and child, and it holds only after a real uncertainty.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ul-02, cf-hu-04. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (closure), D (synecdoche).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-wi-02 anticipatory title

```text
handle: Widow | axis: outward | imposed by: a verdict given before the event
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The title assigns the speaker a status the events have not conferred.

**Surface.** A title that names a condition the body of the poem shows only as fear.

**Fold.** The title sets the premise the speaker lives under, so every line reads as a rehearsal of what the title says has already happened.

**Active site.** The title.

**Forbidden.** Confirming or refuting the title in the body.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know whether the title will come true.

**Stake.** Moderate. It requires a real possibility. A title that cannot come true is a pun.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-wi-03.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (title as frame).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-wi-03 rehearsed rescue

```text
handle: Widow | axis: outward | imposed by: an interval the speaker can fill only by imagining
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker can do nothing about the absent person except imagine harm and then imagine undoing it.

**Surface.** Imagined disasters, each followed by an imagined rescue in the same deliberate manner, ending with a surplus of the person she fears losing.

**Fold.** Treating harm and rescue as equal acts of imagination shows the reader that her love and her dread are the same labor. The surplus at the close is the dread overcompensating.

**Active site.** The first imagined rescue.

**Forbidden.** An actual rescue. Real news. A stated moral.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know the outcome. She controls only her imagining.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the absence has real risk behind it.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-wi-02, cf-hl-03. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (list syntax, parallelism), A (tense shift).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-te-02 bodily knowledge

```text
handle: Termination | axis: upward | imposed by: knowledge that lives in the body and cannot be spoken
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The subject knows something through his body that cannot be put into words or handed on.

**Surface.** The knowledge rendered at length as sensation, touch, hearing, and tremor, and never stated as a proposition.

**Fold.** Keeping the knowledge sensory proves it real and makes it untransmittable in the same stroke. The reader believes it, and sees why no one above him can.

**Active site.** The moment the body registers what it knows.

**Forbidden.** Stating what he knows. Teaching it. A manual.

**Knowledge test.** No one else can know what he knows, and he cannot say it.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a real skill with real consequences.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-te-03, cf-ap-03. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Body-cost rule.

**Device cluster.** A (concretion spike), C (cumulative sentence), G (embodied attention quality).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-te-03 foresight that stops at the self

```text
handle: Termination | axis: upward | imposed by: an expertise that cannot turn on its owner
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The subject can foresee every failure in his field except his own.

**Surface.** His foresight established as total over the objects of his work, and his own breakdown then told as something he never registers.

**Fold.** The expert's range measures his blind spot. The reader sees the accident he cannot hear coming.

**Active site.** The first sign of his breakdown, told in the same terms as his machines.

**Forbidden.** Self-awareness. A warning heeded. Irony at his expense.

**Knowledge test.** He can know the machines' futures and not his own.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds when his expertise is real and the threat to him comes from outside it.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-te-02, cf-jc-03.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** D (simile), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-hi-02 photographer outside the frame

```text
handle: History Lesson | axis: sideways | imposed by: a knower present only as the one who takes the picture
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The person who knows the history is outside the image, present only as the source of its instructions.

**Surface.** The photographer placed behind the camera, heard directing the pose, never seen.

**Fold.** Putting the knower outside the frame makes the image her lesson and her silence at once: she shapes what the child will carry and never says why.

**Active site.** The placement of the photographer.

**Forbidden.** The photographer entering the frame. Her explanation.

**Knowledge test.** The child in the picture cannot know what the photographer knows.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds when the photographer's knowledge is the poem's real subject.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-hi-03, cf-sm-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Access-asymmetry.

**Device cluster.** A (speaker position), E (ekphrastic frame), G (withholding).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-hi-03 repeated pose

```text
handle: History Lesson | axis: sideways | imposed by: a history that can pass only as posture
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** A history that cannot be told to the child passes to her as a pose she repeats without knowing it.

**Surface.** Two images described in parallel, the same posture in different circumstances, with the interval between them stated as dates.

**Fold.** The repeated pose carries what neither image shows alone, and the reader reads the history in the rhyme.

**Active site.** The second image's echo of the first.

**Forbidden.** Stating the history. A third image. A deviation that breaks the rhyme.

**Knowledge test.** The one repeating the pose cannot know what it repeats.

**Stake.** High. It needs a real history between the two poses. Without one it is a family resemblance.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-hi-02.

**Engines admitted.** Access-asymmetry.

**Device cluster.** B (repetition), E (ekphrastic frame, closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ss-02 self-turned sight

```text
handle: Subtle Sight | axis: inward | imposed by: a power of exposure that cannot exempt its user
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker's power to see through others, turned on himself, finds the same thing, and he loses the ground he judged from.

**Surface.** A catalog of exposures of others, then a single turn applying the same exposure to the speaker, stated flatly.

**Fold.** Including himself turns contempt into doubt. The reader can no longer read the catalog as the world's guilt, only as the seer's condition.

**Active site.** The turn.

**Forbidden.** Exempting the speaker. Self-pity. A second turn back outward.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker can know himself only by the same sight, so he cannot know whether his judgment of himself is truer than his judgment of others.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a real catalog of exposure first, or the self-turn is modesty.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ss-03, cf-cl-04. Cancels with cf-sc-05: one speaker sees himself through, and the other cannot see himself at all.

**Engines admitted.** Transparent argument as a phase tension.

**Device cluster.** G (self-implication), C (list syntax).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ss-03 prayer for blindness

```text
handle: Subtle Sight | axis: inward | imposed by: a faculty that can be escaped only by losing it
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker can end his doubt only by asking to lose the faculty that produces it.

**Surface.** A closing petition to have the sight taken away, weighing trust that may be betrayed against doubt that may be confirmed, and choosing the first.

**Fold.** Preferring possible deception to possible truth tells the reader what the sight has cost: the speaker would rather be wrong about the world in company than right about it alone.

**Active site.** The weighing of betrayed trust against confirmed doubt.

**Forbidden.** The prayer granted. A reason from doctrine. Irony.

**Knowledge test.** He cannot know whether the sight is true, and the prayer asks never to find out.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds only where the sight has cost something on the page.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ss-02, cf-hu-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (imperative), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pn-02 involuntary sign

```text
handle: Proud Name | axis: inward | imposed by: a will that governs speech and not the face
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker can know the other's feeling only through signs he is trying to suppress.

**Surface.** Repeated moments where the other's eyes contradict his conduct and are named as betraying him, while his words and bearing stay controlled.

**Fold.** Locating his truth in what he cannot control makes every controlled act evidence of the feeling it hides. The reader trusts the eyes over the man.

**Active site.** The first naming of the eyes as betrayers.

**Forbidden.** His confession in words. The speaker doubting her reading. A third party confirming it.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know his feeling except through signs he suppresses, and she cannot confirm them.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs a real will set against the feeling.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pn-03, cf-pn-04, cf-wa-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** A (concretion spike), G (credibility management).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pn-03 unnamed inhibitor

```text
handle: Proud Name | axis: inward | imposed by: a prohibition the speaker feels and cannot identify
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker knows what she should say and cannot say it, and cannot name what forbids her.

**Surface.** The fault admitted inwardly, the refusal to confess stated, and the forbidding force named only by alternatives, left undecided.

**Fold.** Leaving the inhibitor unnamed makes it larger than either name. The reader watches a will obey something it cannot identify, which is how the loss becomes inevitable.

**Active site.** The undecided naming of what forbids her.

**Forbidden.** Naming it. Confessing. Explaining the refusal.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know what stops her.

**Stake.** High. The silence has to cost the relation on the page.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pn-02, cf-pn-04. Cancels with cf-ap-04: both hold two readings level, and the second adds nothing.

**Engines admitted.** Compartmentalization.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation, modal qualification), G (withholding).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pn-04 parenthetical leak

```text
handle: Proud Name | axis: inward | imposed by: a speaker who cannot say aloud what she knows
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** What the speaker cannot say in the lyric's main voice escapes into asides.

**Surface.** Parenthetical lines interrupting the lyric, carrying admissions, doubts, and judgments the main sentences do not make.

**Fold.** The parentheses are a second voice the speaker cannot suppress. The reader hears her pride in the main line and her truth in the aside.

**Active site.** The first parenthetical admission.

**Forbidden.** Folding the aside into the main voice. Addressing the aside to the beloved.

**Knowledge test.** The people around her cannot know what the asides say. Only the reader hears them.

**Stake.** Moderate. It is a mannerism where the main voice is under no real constraint.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pn-02, cf-pn-03, cf-wa-03.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

**Device cluster.** C (parenthesis), A (pronoun shift).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-kh-02 disarmed plea

```text
handle: Known Heaven | axis: upward | imposed by: a judge before whom the speaker has renounced her defenses
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker has given up every defense before her judge and has only her question left.

**Surface.** A confession and a refusal to accuse, both stated just before the final question.

**Fold.** Renouncing defense and accusation makes the question pure. It cannot be heard as special pleading, so it lands as a charge against the arrangement itself.

**Active site.** The refusal to accuse, immediately before the question.

**Forbidden.** A defense after the confession. An accusation. An answer.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know the verdict, and she has removed every means of influencing it.

**Stake.** High. It needs a real judge and a real guilt.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-kh-03, cf-cl-04. Cancels with cf-hu-02, which forbids the indicative claim about the self that the confession is.

**Engines admitted.** Transparent argument as a phase tension.

**Device cluster.** G (self-implication), C (declarative flatness).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-kh-03 comparative question

```text
handle: Known Heaven | axis: upward | imposed by: a verdict between two parties that only a third can give
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker asks her judge to weigh her guilt against another's, a comparison only the judge can make.

**Surface.** A closing question naming both parties and asking which is guiltier.

**Fold.** Making the question comparative puts the other party on trial beside her without accusing her, and the reader has to weigh them too.

**Active site.** The question.

**Forbidden.** Answering. Hinting at the answer. A third party.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know the answer. Only the judge can.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It holds where both guilts are real on the page.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-kh-02. Cancels with cf-he-03 and cf-wh-04: all three must close on an unanswered question, and a trial closes once.

**Engines admitted.** Transparent argument as a phase tension.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pd-02 narrowing certainty

```text
handle: Path of Dreams | axis: outward | imposed by: a series of limits, each tighter than the last
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The poem's knowledge shrinks at each stage until only one fact remains.

**Surface.** Parallel sections under a fixed frame, each ending on a certainty smaller than the last.

**Fold.** The shrinking certainty is the argument. By the last section the reader holds the one fact that survives all the others.

**Active site.** The close of the last section.

**Forbidden.** A section that widens. A break in the frame. Commentary.

**Knowledge test.** At each stage the poem knows less, and at the end it knows only that it cannot follow.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs sections that are genuinely parallel, or the narrowing reads as fatigue.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pd-03, cf-sb-03, cf-cl-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (parallelism), E (serial sequence).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pd-03 one-way path

```text
handle: Path of Dreams | axis: outward | imposed by: a destination from which no traveler reports
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The poem can describe a road and its travelers but never its end, because no one who reaches the end comes back.

**Surface.** A path described in detail up to a threshold, the destination given only as alternatives, and non-return stated as the one certainty.

**Fold.** Stating non-return as knowledge turns ignorance into a fact the reader can hold.

**Active site.** The statement of non-return.

**Forbidden.** A returned traveler. A vision of the destination. A choice between the alternatives.

**Knowledge test.** No one on the path can know its end.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds when the path has been made real before its end is withheld.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pd-02, cf-no-04. Cancels with cf-ap-02: one holds that someone could answer and is gone, the other that no one comes back with the answer.

**Engines admitted.** Load-bearing foreignness.

**Device cluster.** D (allegory), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-yh-02 dead rival

```text
handle: Yonder Hill | axis: sideways | imposed by: a rival death has fixed and removed
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker competes with someone who cannot be met, changed, or outlived.

**Surface.** The rival's silence and absence named at the start, then the rival felt as a presence in the speaker's intimacy and surroundings.

**Fold.** Death makes the rival perfect and everywhere at once. The reader sees the contest cannot be won, because it has no second party.

**Active site.** The first intrusion of the rival into the present.

**Forbidden.** The rival's faults. Her voice. The husband's comparison of the two.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know the rival, or what she still means to him.

**Stake.** High. It needs a real love on both sides.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-yh-03, cf-pn-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** D (personification), A (spatial deixis).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-yh-03 wish to be the lost

```text
handle: Yonder Hill | axis: sideways | imposed by: a contest only the lost can win
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker sees one way to win the love she wants: to be the one who is gone.

**Surface.** A closing wish to exchange places with the dead.

**Fold.** The wish exposes the contest's logic. The speaker would give up her life to have what death gave the rival.

**Active site.** The wish.

**Forbidden.** The wish granted, argued, or retracted.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know whether being lost would win the love. She knows only that the lost one has it.

**Stake.** High. Where the love is slight, the wish is melodrama.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-yh-02, cf-mc-03. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (closure), C (exclamatory charge).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-nh-02 refrain turned

```text
handle: Not Hear | axis: outward | imposed by: a question the speaker can answer only by deciding it
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker has one question she cannot answer, and the poem ends by answering it anyway.

**Surface.** A refrain line returns at the close of a later section with the same words and a changed mood, question become statement.

**Fold.** Holding the words fixed and changing only the mood makes the change the finding. The reader sees exactly how much the speaker has learned, which is nothing, and how much she has decided, which is everything.

**Active site.** The returned refrain.

**Forbidden.** Changing the words. A third return. Evidence between the two.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know the answer. She can only decide it.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs a question that matters and no evidence to settle it.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-nh-03, cf-cl-02. Cancels with cf-he-03, which forbids answering the question the refrain answers.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** B (refrain), E (refrain shift).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-nh-03 attention elsewhere

```text
handle: Not Hear | axis: outward | imposed by: an addressee absorbed in something the speaker cannot share
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker explains the addressee's silence by an attention now given to something greater, which also means the speaker no longer matters to the addressee.

**Surface.** A closing explanation that the addressee now hears a higher music and has set former loves aside.

**Fold.** The explanation consoles and excludes in the same clause. The reader hears the speaker accept a heaven with no room in it for her.

**Active site.** The explanation.

**Forbidden.** Bitterness. Hope of return. The addressee's confirmation.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know what the addressee attends to. The explanation is her supposition.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It holds where the speaker's love is real and the consolation costs her.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-nh-02, cf-mc-03.

**Engines admitted.** Load-bearing foreignness.

**Device cluster.** D (allusion), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pw-02 borrowed eyes

```text
handle: Prisoned Within | axis: sideways | imposed by: a self-image dictated by those who reject the speaker
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker can describe himself only in the terms of those who reject him.

**Surface.** A self-description given as a catalog of the features others see, in their evaluative vocabulary, set against a claim of an unseen inner self.

**Fold.** Adopting the observers' vocabulary shows how far their view has entered him, and the inner self is asserted against his own words.

**Active site.** The first self-description in the observers' terms.

**Forbidden.** A neutral self-description. Rejecting the terms. A witness who sees otherwise.

**Knowledge test.** He cannot see himself except as they see him.

**Stake.** High. The vocabulary may carry real contempt for real groups, and a trial must not turn it on them.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pw-03, cf-ss-02. Cancels with cf-sc-05: one speaker knows he is using others' terms, and the other cannot see his register.

**Engines admitted.** Anti-laundering engine default.

**Device cluster.** C (list syntax, apposition), G (self-implication, appropriation risk).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pw-03 body as answer

```text
handle: Prisoned Within | axis: sideways | imposed by: an appearance that answers for the speaker before anyone else can
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** Every question the speaker asks about his prospects is answered by his own appearance before anyone else can answer it.

**Surface.** Rhetorical questions about what he can win or know, each followed at once by a description of his body in apposition.

**Fold.** The apposition makes the body the verdict. No one has to refuse him, because his face already has.

**Active site.** The first apposition.

**Forbidden.** An answer from anyone else. A question left open.

**Knowledge test.** He cannot know how anyone would answer, since his body answers first.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It holds where the appearance is really punished. Otherwise it is self-pity.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pw-02, cf-cl-02.

**Engines admitted.** Body-cost rule.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation, apposition).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-og-02 sensory leakage

```text
handle: Outer Gate | axis: upward | imposed by: walls that let only light, sound, and scent through
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker knows an inside only through what escapes it.

**Surface.** The inside rendered entirely through leakage, light through curtains, sound through walls, scent on the air, and never seen directly.

**Fold.** Leakage gives the outline exactly and the substance not at all. The reader builds the feast from its leaks and feels the speaker's hunger in the building.

**Active site.** The first leak.

**Forbidden.** A direct view. Entry. An insider's report.

**Knowledge test.** He cannot know the inside except through what leaks out.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the inside is really closed to him.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-og-03, cf-sb-02, cf-jc-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** A (spatial deixis), D (synecdoche).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-og-03 grateful scrap

```text
handle: Outer Gate | axis: upward | imposed by: an exclusion that permits only the smallest share
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The excluded can hope only for the smallest share, and would be grateful for it.

**Surface.** The outsider's thanks for any least portion flung out, stated before the application to the speaker's own case, where even that portion does not come.

**Fold.** The gratitude calibrates the exclusion. The reader measures the lover's loss by the scrap the starving man would have been glad of.

**Active site.** The gratitude.

**Forbidden.** A scrap received by the speaker. A demand. Bitterness.

**Knowledge test.** He cannot know whether any share will come.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the share is really withheld.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-og-02, cf-hu-02.

**Engines admitted.** Body-cost rule.

**Device cluster.** D (simile), G (credibility management).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-lp-02 prescribed beloved

```text
handle: Love Passed By | axis: inward | imposed by: an ideal assembled from inherited models
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker has specified the loved one in advance from inherited models, and the specification blinds.

**Surface.** A detailed advance description, feature by feature, in the idiom of songs and stories. Then an arrival that matches none of it.

**Fold.** The reader checks the arrival against the prescription and sees the mismatch first, so the blindness is visible before the speaker feels it.

**Active site.** The arrival's first mismatched feature.

**Forbidden.** A partial match. A revised prescription. The speaker noticing in time.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know what does not match the model.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It holds where the models really govern the speaker.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-lp-03, cf-ph-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (list syntax, parallelism), D (allusion).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-lp-03 failed announcer

```text
handle: Love Passed By | axis: inward | imposed by: a faculty trusted to recognize the thing, which does not
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker trusts one faculty to announce the thing when it comes, and it stays silent.

**Surface.** An early promise that the faculty will announce the thing, and a late accusation of the faculty after the thing has passed.

**Fold.** The faculty's silence locates the failure inside the speaker. The reader sees that the thing arrived and the instrument missed it.

**Active site.** The accusation.

**Forbidden.** An outside excuse. The faculty vindicated. A second arrival.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know the thing except through the faculty, and the faculty fails.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the speaker has no other way to know.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-lp-02, cf-te-03.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (parenthesis), E (turn).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ws-02 conscious corpse

```text
handle: Winding Sheet | axis: outward | imposed by: a death the speaker narrates from inside
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** The speaker narrates her own death from inside it, aware and unheard.

**Surface.** The rites performed on her body reported in the first person, with the mourners' belief in her absence quoted.

**Fold.** Narrating from inside the death makes the reader her only listener. The mourners' verdict is heard as an error only the reader can see.

**Active site.** The mourners' quoted verdict.

**Forbidden.** Being heard. A return to life. Horror.

**Knowledge test.** The mourners cannot know she is aware.

**Stake.** Moderate. Without a secret to keep, it is a gothic device.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ws-03. Cancels with cf-pd-03: one speaks from beyond death, and the other holds that no one reports back.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** A (speaker position), D (quotation).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ws-03 unwitting courier

```text
handle: Winding Sheet | axis: outward | imposed by: a token delivered by someone who cannot read it
rights ceiling: Level 4 available, held at Level 0
```

**Position.** A token carrying a secret meaning is delivered by someone who does not know what it means.

**Surface.** A third party places a token from the secret beloved on the speaker, with the beloved marked only by an emphasis the third party cannot see.

**Fold.** The courier's ignorance protects the secret while delivering it. The reader watches the secret pass through hands that would stop it if they knew.

**Active site.** The placing of the token.

**Forbidden.** The courier noticing. Naming the beloved. Explanation.

**Knowledge test.** The courier cannot know what the token means.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It holds where discovery would cost someone.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ws-02, cf-wa-04. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

**Device cluster.** F (typographic emphasis), G (withholding).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pu-02 custodial voice

```text
handle: Punctum | axis: upward | imposed by: a plural voice that controls how an image is seen
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** Those who control the image speak for it, steering the viewer away from what it hides.

**Surface.** A plural voice, reported as speech, giving the viewer directions in the second person: look here, it is nothing, move on.

**Fold.** The voice's directions trace the outline of what they avoid. The reader learns what is hidden from where the voice refuses to point.

**Active site.** The voice's explanation of the trace as a flaw.

**Forbidden.** The voice confessing. The poem silencing it. Its explanations rebutted.

**Knowledge test.** The viewer cannot know what the image hides except by where the voice will not let the eye go.

**Stake.** High. It needs a real harm behind the curation.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pu-03, cf-hi-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Anti-laundering engine default.

**Device cluster.** C (reported speech, imperative), G (tonal laundering).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-pu-03 orphan shadow

```text
handle: Punctum | axis: upward | imposed by: a removal that could not take everything
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The one thing the removal left behind is a trace attached to nothing.

**Surface.** A shadow or mark returned to repeatedly, described exactly, and owned by no visible body.

**Fold.** The orphan trace is proof of what was removed. Its exactness makes the absence the most present thing in the image.

**Active site.** The first return to the trace.

**Forbidden.** Filling the trace. Explaining it. A second trace.

**Knowledge test.** The viewer cannot see the body, only its shadow.

**Stake.** High. It needs a removal that was an act, not an accident.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-pu-02, cf-mt-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** D (synecdoche), G (withholding).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ah-02 ledger life

```text
handle: Amazon History | axis: outward | imposed by: a record that holds only transactions
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** A life is told only through a transactional record, in the record's own format.

**Surface.** Entries in the document's format, dated, quantified, and uncommented, forming the whole poem.

**Fold.** The record's refusal to feel makes the reader do the feeling. Every inference is the reader's.

**Active site.** The first entry that changes what the earlier entries mean.

**Forbidden.** Commentary. A speaker. An entry the record would not contain.

**Knowledge test.** The record cannot know what the purchases meant, and the reader can only infer.

**Stake.** High. It needs a record whose items can carry a life.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ah-03, cf-ah-04, cf-jc-02, cf-me-04. Cancels with cf-wl-02: a poem outsources its load-bearing statement once, and here the whole poem is found text.

**Engines admitted.** Body-cost rule.

**Device cluster.** C (list syntax), F (document format).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ah-03 blank entry

```text
handle: Amazon History | axis: outward | imposed by: a record's silence where nothing was recorded
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The most important information is where the record is empty.

**Surface.** A dated heading with no entries under it, placed among full ones, uncommented.

**Fold.** The empty period makes the reader supply what happened when nothing was bought. The silence is louder than any item.

**Active site.** The first empty period.

**Forbidden.** Explaining the blank. Filling it. Marking it beyond the record's own format.

**Knowledge test.** The reader cannot know what happened in the blank.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a record whose blanks can mean.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ah-02, cf-ah-04, cf-mt-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** F (document format), G (omission).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ah-04 posthumous entries

```text
handle: Amazon History | axis: outward | imposed by: a record that outlives the person it recorded
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The record goes on after its subject dies, and its entries change owner without saying so.

**Surface.** Entries continuing after the memorial items, for purposes the record does not state.

**Fold.** The continuation turns the document from her life to a survivor's without the document noticing. The reader sees grief as a line item.

**Active site.** The first entry after the memorial goods.

**Forbidden.** Naming the buyer. Explaining the purchases. Ending with the death.

**Knowledge test.** The reader cannot know who is buying, or for whom.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a subject whose death is on the record.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ah-02, cf-ah-03, cf-ws-03.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (closure), F (document format).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ol-02 idiom stack

```text
handle: Old Glory | axis: sideways | imposed by: a language whose commonplaces carry violence
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The poem uses only a culture's commonplace phrases, and the phrases carry what their users do not hear.

**Surface.** Phrases stacked without comment, each an idiom in ordinary use, all sharing a buried literal field.

**Fold.** Accumulation revives the dead metaphor. The reader hears the literal sense rise with each phrase.

**Active site.** The phrase at which the literal sense can no longer be ignored.

**Forbidden.** Comment. Invented phrases. A phrase outside the field.

**Knowledge test.** The users cannot hear the literal sense, and the poem cannot say it.

**Stake.** High. It needs a literal field with real victims.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ol-03, cf-as-02. See Section D. Counts as an unknowing-register primitive, so it cancels with cf-sc-05 and cf-jc-04.

**Engines admitted.** Anti-laundering engine default.

**Device cluster.** C (parataxis, list syntax), D (dead metaphor).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ol-03 title as the only frame

```text
handle: Old Glory | axis: sideways | imposed by: a poem that allows itself no comment but its title
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The poem's only statement in its own voice is its title.

**Surface.** A title that names a larger frame, a flag or an institution, over a body with no authorial comment.

**Fold.** The title turns collage into testimony. Everything below reads as evidence about what the title names.

**Active site.** The title.

**Forbidden.** A second frame. An explaining epigraph. Comment in the body.

**Knowledge test.** The body cannot know what the title makes of it. Only the reader connects the two.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs a body that can bear the title's weight without help.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ol-02, cf-ah-02. Cancels with cf-wi-02: a poem has one title.

**Engines admitted.** Transparent ugliness.

**Device cluster.** E (title as frame).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-sh-02 stranger's diagnosis

```text
handle: Shaking Hands | axis: inward | imposed by: a symptom visible to others before the self
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** Someone else sees the speaker's condition before he does, and names it as a question.

**Surface.** A brief exchange in which a stranger's questions reveal a symptom the speaker had not noticed.

**Fold.** The stranger becomes the first witness to the speaker's state. The reader learns his condition from outside before he does.

**Active site.** The question that names the symptom.

**Forbidden.** The stranger explaining. The speaker already knowing. Intimacy.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know his own state until someone else names it.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the symptom matters.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-sh-03, cf-pw-02.

**Engines admitted.** Body-cost rule.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation, dialogue), A (speaker position).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-sh-03 sourceless feeling

```text
handle: Shaking Hands | axis: inward | imposed by: a feeling that survives its cause
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker keeps a feeling and loses what caused it.

**Surface.** A sensation or pleasure registered precisely, with its source forgotten at once and stated plainly.

**Fold.** A feeling without its source becomes the model for the whole condition. The reader sees a life that has come loose from its causes.

**Active site.** The forgetting.

**Forbidden.** Recovering the source. Regret stated.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know where the feeling came from.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the loss of source mirrors a larger loss.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-sh-02, cf-no-03. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (open ending), G (embodied attention quality).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ps-02 work instead of grief

```text
handle: Passed a Soul | axis: inward | imposed by: a feeling the speaker will not allow
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker substitutes a task for a feeling he refuses, and does the task thoroughly.

**Surface.** Physical work rendered in close detail at the time the feeling would come, with its purpose stated once.

**Fold.** The detail of the task is where the grief goes. The reader measures the feeling by the diligence of its substitute.

**Active site.** The statement of the work's purpose.

**Forbidden.** The feeling breaking through. A second statement of purpose. The task failing.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker will not let himself know the feeling.

**Stake.** High. It needs a feeling the speaker has real reason to refuse.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ps-03, cf-ap-03, cf-te-02.

**Engines admitted.** Compartmentalization.

**Device cluster.** A (concretion spike), C (declarative flatness).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ps-03 unanswered summons

```text
handle: Passed a Soul | axis: inward | imposed by: a call the speaker lets ring
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The world the speaker is avoiding calls, and he does not answer.

**Surface.** A signal that sounds, stops, and sounds again, left unanswered, once, midway.

**Fold.** The unanswered call is the only intrusion of what he refuses, and it marks where the event is happening without him.

**Active site.** The second sounding.

**Forbidden.** Answering. Identifying the caller. A third sounding.

**Knowledge test.** He cannot know who calls or why, and chooses not to.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs an event the call could only be about.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ps-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (interruption), G (withholding).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ea-02 displaced catalog

```text
handle: Enueg I | axis: inward | imposed by: an object the speaker will not look at
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker catalogs everything except the one thing he fled, and each item takes on some of its weight.

**Surface.** An inventory of vexations met in sequence, each introduced as the next, after a single opening mention of what was left behind.

**Fold.** Displacement spreads the thing not looked at across the whole catalog. The reader sees it in every item.

**Active site.** The first item that resembles what was left.

**Forbidden.** Returning to the fled object. Naming the resemblance. A neutral item.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot look at the thing, so he knows it only as what everything else resembles.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a fled object with real weight.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ea-03, cf-ps-02.

**Engines admitted.** Complaint loop.

**Device cluster.** C (list syntax), E (serial sequence).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ea-03 learned exclusion

```text
handle: Enueg I | axis: inward | imposed by: a refusal remembered from before
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** A figure met on the way will not enter where he is now welcome, because he was once put out.

**Surface.** A brief exchange with a child or stranger who declines an invitation and gives the earlier expulsion as the reason, knowingly.

**Fold.** The figure's knowledge of exclusion mirrors the speaker's position without comment. The reader sees the speaker's avoidance explained by someone else's.

**Active site.** The reason given.

**Forbidden.** The speaker drawing the parallel. Persuading the figure. The figure entering.

**Knowledge test.** The figure cannot know he would be let in now. He knows only that he was put out before.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the speaker's own avoidance is real.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ea-02, cf-og-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** A (reported speech), D (analogy by juxtaposition).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-eb-02 truncated formula

```text
handle: Enueg II | axis: sideways | imposed by: a ritual phrase the speaker cannot finish
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker begins a fixed formula that decorum supplies and cannot finish it.

**Surface.** A known proverb or liturgical phrase, quoted or altered, broken off before its last word, without comment.

**Fold.** The reader completes the formula silently and hears the word the speaker could not say. The missing word carries the poem's attitude without being said.

**Active site.** The break.

**Forbidden.** Completing it. Explaining it. A formula the reader will not know.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot say the word the formula requires.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a real occasion for the formula.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-eb-03, cf-ca-04. Counts as a refusal primitive under the Section D ceiling, so it cancels with cf-sc-02, cf-sb-04, cf-wa-04, cf-pr-02, and cf-me-03.

**Engines admitted.** Load-bearing foreignness.

**Device cluster.** C (aposiopesis), G (withholding).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-eb-03 plea for the imprint

```text
handle: Enueg II | axis: sideways | imposed by: a face that will not stay
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker asks for the one legendary image that keeps a face after its bearer is gone, and cannot have it.

**Surface.** A plea, in a religious or legendary figure, for an imprint of a face, set beside a face that is dissolving.

**Fold.** The plea names the kind of preservation the poem cannot perform, and the dissolving face is what the poem has instead.

**Active site.** The plea.

**Forbidden.** The imprint granted. A description of the face holding.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot keep the face. The image that would keep it belongs to legend.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the face matters.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-eb-02, cf-pu-03. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Load-bearing foreignness.

**Device cluster.** D (allusion), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-al-02 opaque presence

```text
handle: Alba | axis: outward | imposed by: a presence that is only itself
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** What the speaker waited for arrives and discloses nothing beyond itself.

**Surface.** An arrival described first by everything it will bring, then as a surface that states only itself.

**Fold.** The presence's completeness is the obstacle. The reader sees the promise cancelled by its own fulfillment.

**Active site.** The moment the presence becomes a surface.

**Forbidden.** A disclosure. A second arrival. Blame.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know anything through the presence beyond the presence.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a real expectation of revelation.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-al-03, cf-lp-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** D (metaphor), E (turn).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-al-03 negated sacrament

```text
handle: Alba | axis: outward | imposed by: a revelation the occasion promised and cannot deliver
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The revelations the occasion should bring are named only to be denied.

**Surface.** A short series of negations naming, one at a time, the illumination, disclosure, and sacrament the occasion should bring.

**Fold.** Naming what does not come makes the absence exact. The reader counts the losses in the order the genre promised them.

**Active site.** The series.

**Forbidden.** A partial yes. A substitute. Lament.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot receive what the occasion promised.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs a genre whose promises the reader knows.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-al-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (negation, parallelism).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ma-02 rationed seeing

```text
handle: Malacoda | axis: sideways | imposed by: a witness the speaker must protect and cannot shield for long
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker controls what another may hear and see, and the control shrinks.

**Surface.** A refrain of permissions, varied at each return, each variation removing one protection, ending when none is left.

**Fold.** The variations count down the protection. The reader feels the moment approach through grammar alone.

**Active site.** The last variation.

**Forbidden.** The protected person's voice. A variation that restores protection. Explanation.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot keep the other from knowing. He can only delay it.

**Stake.** High. It needs a sight that will really cost the protected person.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ma-03, cf-ma-04, cf-nh-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** B (refrain with variation), G (withholding).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ma-03 hired devils

```text
handle: Malacoda | axis: sideways | imposed by: professionals whose office turns a death into procedure
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The family's grief is handled by paid professionals whose composure is part of the job, and the speaker cannot interfere.

**Surface.** The professionals given an infernal or mythic name, their composure and payment noted, their work given as procedure.

**Fold.** Naming them as devils while showing only their competence makes the reader feel the family's view of an ordinary job. The poem condemns nothing and names everything.

**Active site.** The naming.

**Forbidden.** Professional cruelty. Condemnation. A professional's inner life.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know what the professionals feel. Their composure is paid for.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the procedure touches a real loss.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ma-02, cf-ma-04.

**Engines admitted.** Load-bearing foreignness.

**Device cluster.** D (allusion), A (third-person management).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ma-04 last refusal

```text
handle: Malacoda | axis: sideways | imposed by: a process the speaker cannot stop
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker's only act against an irreversible process is a refusal spoken after it has happened.

**Surface.** A collective assent, then a single-word refusal as the last line.

**Fold.** The refusal can change nothing, and that is its meaning. The reader hears the whole grief in a word with no effect.

**Active site.** The last word.

**Forbidden.** A second word. The refusal heeded. Explanation.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker knows the refusal changes nothing.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a process that really cannot be stopped.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ma-02, cf-ma-03.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-dt-02 proxy farewell

```text
handle: Da Tagte Es | axis: outward | imposed by: a goodbye that could not be said to its object
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker's farewells went to substitutes, and the real one was never said.

**Surface.** A reference to substitute goodbyes, with an imperative asking the absent one to make them good.

**Fold.** Asking the absent to redeem the substitutes names the missing goodbye by its proxies. The reader hears what was never said.

**Active site.** The imperative.

**Forbidden.** The real goodbye. A list of the substitutes. An answer.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know whether the substitutes reached him.

**Stake.** High. It needs a farewell that really could not be made.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-dt-03, cf-cl-03, cf-ws-03.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (imperative), D (metonymy).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-dt-03 null reading

```text
handle: Da Tagte Es | axis: outward | imposed by: an instrument whose absence of result is the evidence
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The decisive fact is known only because an instrument shows nothing.

**Surface.** A test or instrument named at the close with its null result, and no statement of what the result means.

**Fold.** The nothing on the instrument is the whole announcement. The reader turns absence into the fact.

**Active site.** The null result.

**Forbidden.** Stating the fact. A second test. A positive reading.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker knows the fact only by what fails to show.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a fact the speaker cannot bear to state.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-dt-02. Cancels with cf-mt-03 and cf-ul-02: all three locate a limit by the reach of the means of knowing.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** D (metonymy), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ec-02 true judge below

```text
handle: Echo's Bones | axis: outward | imposed by: a judgment only the consumers can make
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The only accurate judgment of the subject belongs to what consumes it.

**Surface.** A closing assignment of true judgment to a non-human consumer.

**Fold.** Moving judgment to the consumer indicts every living judgment.

**Active site.** The assignment.

**Forbidden.** A human judge. Mercy. Argument.

**Knowledge test.** The living cannot know the subject as the consumer does.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where living judgment of the dead is at stake.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ec-03. Cancels with cf-mt-04, cf-ap-02, and cf-wl-02, since a poem outsources its load-bearing knowledge once.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (closure), D (synecdoche).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ec-03 heard through the ground

```text
handle: Echo's Bones | axis: outward | imposed by: a boundary through which the dead can only be overheard
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker perceives the dead only as muffled sound through the ground.

**Surface.** The dead rendered as festivity heard through a barrier while the speaker walks above.

**Fold.** The festive figure makes decay a party the living are not invited to. The reader hears the boundary.

**Active site.** The first sounding.

**Forbidden.** Seeing through. Joining in. Horror.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot perceive the dead except muffled.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the barrier is final.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ec-02, cf-og-02.

**Engines admitted.** Defamiliarization engine.

**Device cluster.** A (spatial deixis), D (metaphor).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ca-02 worn words

```text
handle: Cascando | axis: inward | imposed by: a vocabulary exhausted by use
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker has only words worn past meaning for the thing he must say.

**Surface.** An explicit judgment that the words are worn out, followed by their continued use, often repeated.

**Fold.** Admitting the wear and using the words anyway makes each use a confession of insufficiency. The reader hears the feeling in the distrust.

**Active site.** The judgment that the words are worn out.

**Forbidden.** A fresh word. Abandoning the words. Irony that exempts the speaker.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know whether the words still carry what he means.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a feeling that matters more than the words.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ca-03, cf-ca-04. Cancels with cf-ph-03: both disown the poem's own medium, and the second adds nothing.

**Engines admitted.** Complaint loop.

**Device cluster.** B (repetition), G (credibility management).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ca-03 unverifiable sincerity

```text
handle: Cascando | axis: inward | imposed by: a feeling the speaker cannot verify from inside
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker cannot tell whether he feels, knows, or pretends.

**Surface.** Fears listed in widening order, ending in the fear of pretense, and the last fear repeated.

**Fold.** Including pretense among the fears means the poem cannot vouch for its own sincerity, and it says so. The reader has to decide whether a poem afraid of insincerity is insincere.

**Active site.** The repeated fear.

**Forbidden.** Reassurance. A test. The beloved's verdict.

**Knowledge test.** He cannot know from inside whether he pretends.

**Stake.** High. It needs a speaker whose sincerity matters to someone.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ca-02, cf-ca-04, cf-ss-02. Cancels with cf-sc-05: one speaker sees that he may be false, and the other cannot see himself at all.

**Engines admitted.** Transparent argument as a phase tension.

**Device cluster.** C (list syntax), G (self-implication).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-ca-04 orphaned condition

```text
handle: Cascando | axis: inward | imposed by: a certainty no one in the poem has
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The poem ends on a condition whose consequence it cannot state.

**Surface.** A final section consisting of one conditional clause with no main clause.

**Fold.** The missing main clause is the missing certainty. The reader waits for a consequence the poem cannot supply.

**Active site.** The final line.

**Forbidden.** Completing the sentence. A second clause. Punctuation that closes it.

**Knowledge test.** The speaker cannot know the outcome the condition depends on.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a condition that decides something real.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-ca-02, cf-ca-03, cf-eb-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (conditionals, syntax), E (open ending).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-oo-02 indiscriminate collection

```text
handle: Ooftish | axis: upward | imposed by: a collector that takes everything alike
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The institution accepts every suffering without distinction, and the lack of distinction is the harm.

**Surface.** A solicitation listing afflictions of every size as equally welcome, with the assurance that all go into one common store.

**Fold.** The equal welcome erases each suffering's particularity. The reader hears care turned into accounting.

**Active site.** The assurance that everything goes in together.

**Forbidden.** A suffering refused. A suffering singled out. The collector's doubt.

**Knowledge test.** The sufferer cannot know what becomes of his particular suffering.

**Stake.** High. It needs an institution with a real claim on the sufferer.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-oo-03, cf-pu-02.

**Engines admitted.** Anti-laundering engine default.

**Device cluster.** C (imperative, list syntax), D (irony).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-oo-03 formula as sense

```text
handle: Ooftish | axis: upward | imposed by: an account of suffering reduced to one phrase
rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The only sense offered for suffering is a single inherited formula.

**Surface.** A closing line in which the institution's promise of meaning is fulfilled by a set phrase.

**Fold.** The promised meaning arrives as a formula, and the gap between promise and phrase is the poem's judgment.

**Active site.** The closing phrase.

**Forbidden.** Explaining the formula. A second formula. The sufferer's reply.

**Knowledge test.** The sufferer cannot know any meaning beyond the formula.

**Stake.** High. It needs a formula with real authority over the sufferer.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-oo-02, cf-nh-03. Cancels with cf-he-03: one closes on a formula, the other on a real question, and a trial closes once.

**Engines admitted.** Transparent ugliness.

**Device cluster.** E (closure), D (irony).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-vu-02 self-cancelling appetite

```text

rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker's want can be satisfied only by a condition that ends the want itself.

**Surface.** A desire deferred explicitly until the moment that would abolish both the desire and the one who desires.

**Fold.** The deferral names annihilation as the only possible satisfaction. The reader sees the want and its end fused.

**Active site.** The deferral.

**Forbidden.** Partial satisfaction. Any other end.

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-vu-03 rising prey

```text

rights ceiling: in copyright, Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** What the speaker needs dead keeps coming back to life.

**Surface.** A descent toward figures that look dead, with the statement that they will rise.

**Fold.** The prey's return to life makes each approach a failure. The reader sees the appetite mocked by life itself.

**Active site.** The statement that they will rise.

**Forbidden.** The prey staying down. The speaker's triumph.

### cf-fc-02 conceded surface


**Position.** The speaker concedes every surface quality to those who shape her, and claims nothing there.

**Surface.** A series of calm concessions about appearance and fortune, placed before a single reserved claim.

### cf-fc-03 kept center

```text
handle: Floating Cake | axis: upward | imposed by: a core formed before the shaping began
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The one thing the speaker holds is her center, which the shaping never reaches.

**Surface.** A closing claim about the inside, in terms of color and constancy, made after everything outside has been given away.

**Fold.** The center is proved by being the only thing not conceded.

**Active site.** The closing claim.

**Forbidden.** Describing the center in detail. Defending it. A second claim.

### cf-ob-02 signed ingredient

```text

rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Surface.** The speaker's own name set inside the description of the gift's preparation, in the third person.

**Fold.** Self-naming makes the gift the self. The reader sees the giver put herself at risk.

### cf-ob-03 fated test

```text

rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The answer the speaker needs will show only as the visible result of a test she sets and cannot read in advance.

**Surface.** A conditional naming the sign that will appear if the answer is yes, and a closing statement that the test changes what it touches.

**Fold.** The test puts the verdict outside her: it will appear in the other, visibly, whatever he says.

**Active site.** The conditional.

**Forbidden.** The result. Cheating the test. A second test.

### cf-um-02 guessing question

```text

rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker asks the listener to guess what she feels, and does not tell.

**Surface.** A direct question about her inner state, early in the poem, left unanswered.

**Fold.** The question marks the one thing she keeps. The reader feels it as held back rather than absent.

**Active site.** The question.

**Forbidden.** Answering. A hint. Self-pity.

**Knowledge test.** The listener cannot know her feeling.

### cf-um-03 riddled verdict

```text
handle: Unwed Mother | axis: sideways | imposed by: a shame the speaker refuses to accept
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker answers the world's verdict with a riddle instead of a defense.

**Surface.** A closing paradox, light in tone, that restates her condition as a puzzle about having and not having.

**Fold.** The riddle's lightness refuses the shame. The reader hears defiance as wit.

**Active site.** The paradox.

**Forbidden.** Explaining it. A defense. An apology.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

### cf-tb-02 fixed innocence

