```text
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The figures of an image are fixed before experience and will stay that way.

**Surface.** The image's permanence projected across very long spans with the figures unchanged, stated as preservation and as deprivation.

**Fold.** Permanence and deprivation are the same fact. The reader sees lasting as a sentence.

**Active site.** The projection across time.

**Forbidden.** Change. The figures' voices.

**Knowledge test.** The figures cannot know anything outside their fixed moment.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where what they are kept from matters.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-tb-03.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** A (temporal deixis), E (ekphrastic frame).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-tb-03 blamed maker

```text
handle: Two Beauties | axis: outward | imposed by: a maker whose limits decide the image
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** What the image lacks is traced to what its maker did not know.

**Surface.** A closing turn from the figures to their maker, charging him with the omission.

**Fold.** Moving the limit to the maker makes the image a portrait of him. The reader learns the maker's ignorance from his work.

**Active site.** The charge.

**Forbidden.** The maker's defense. Praise. A second charge.

**Knowledge test.** The figures cannot know more than the maker put in.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the omission is real.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-tb-02, cf-hi-02, cf-tm-03. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** E (turn, closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-gw-02 borrowed taxonomy

```text
handle: Girl without a Sex | axis: outward | imposed by: a subject no available category fits
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The subject cannot be classified in the terms available, so the poem tries another domain's terms, which fail too.

**Surface.** Classification questions posed in another field's vocabulary, left unanswered.

**Fold.** The borrowed vocabulary shows the failure is in the categories, not in the words.

**Active site.** The second question.

**Forbidden.** An answer. A clinical term. Mockery.

**Knowledge test.** No available category fits the subject.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the classification decides something about her life.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-gw-03. Cancels with cf-ap-04: both hold two kinds level, and the second adds nothing.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation, parallelism), D (metaphor).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-gw-03 exemption as consolation

```text
handle: Girl without a Sex | axis: outward | imposed by: a social role the subject's difference excludes her from
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The only certain consequence of the subject's difference is an exemption from a burden.

**Surface.** A closing consolation naming the role she will never have to fill, delivered with a shrug.

**Fold.** The consolation indicts the role. The reader sees exclusion as rescue.

**Active site.** The consolation.

**Forbidden.** Grief. Pity. The role described favorably.

**Knowledge test.** The only knowable consequence is what she escapes.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a role harsh enough that escape is real.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-gw-02.

**Engines admitted.** Anti-laundering engine default.

**Device cluster.** E (closure), D (irony).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-vt-02 two-way formula

```text
handle: Vinh-Tuong | axis: sideways | imposed by: a required form whose phrases carry two readings
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker must use the set phrases of a ritual form, and chooses phrases that read two ways.

**Surface.** A run of the form's formulas, each with an opposite reading available.

**Fold.** The double readings keep the form correct and the feeling unreadable. The reader cannot convict the speaker of either.

**Active site.** The first formula that turns.

**Forbidden.** A phrase that commits. Comment. A wink.

**Knowledge test.** The reader cannot know which reading the speaker means.

**Stake.** High. It needs a real obligation to the form.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-vt-03. Cancels with cf-ol-02: one speaker chooses her double readings, and the other's voices cannot hear theirs.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

**Device cluster.** D (idiom), G (plausible deniability).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-vt-03 framing cry

```text
handle: Vinh-Tuong | axis: sideways | imposed by: a ritual cry that must open and close the poem
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The lament must begin and end in the prescribed cry, whatever lies between.

**Surface.** The opening line returned unchanged as the closing line.

**Fold.** The returning cry seals the poem in correct form, and everything ambiguous is held inside a frame no one can fault.

**Active site.** The return.

**Forbidden.** Altering the cry. A different close.

**Knowledge test.** The frame cannot tell the reader what the speaker feels.

**Stake.** Moderate. It needs a form that really prescribes the cry.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-vt-02, cf-nh-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** B (returning line), E (frame).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-rs-02 speakable figure

```text
handle: River Snail | axis: sideways | imposed by: a subject that may be spoken only in disguise
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The forbidden subject can be spoken only through an innocent figure whose every detail fits both.

**Surface.** A humble object or creature described exactly, each term apt for the object and for the forbidden subject, neither named as the other.

**Fold.** The exact double fit makes the second meaning undeniable to the reader and unprovable to the censor.

**Active site.** The first term that fits both too well.

**Forbidden.** Naming the second subject. A detail that fits only one.

**Knowledge test.** No one can prove the second meaning, and the speaker can deny it.

**Stake.** High. It needs a real prohibition behind the disguise.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-rs-03, cf-tm-02, cf-as-02. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

**Device cluster.** D (conceit, double entendre), G (plausible deniability).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-rs-03 terms in code

```text
handle: River Snail | axis: sideways | imposed by: a refusal that cannot be spoken plainly
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker sets a limit on what may be done to her, and can set it only inside the disguise.

**Surface.** A grant of permission followed by a refusal of one act, both in the figure's terms.

**Fold.** The code carries consent and refusal exactly while staying deniable. The reader sees that the only place she can bargain is inside the metaphor.

**Active site.** The refusal.

**Forbidden.** Stating the refusal plainly. Withdrawing the permission. Apology.

**Knowledge test.** The listener cannot hold her to the words, and she cannot say them any other way.

**Stake.** High. It needs a speaker with something real to refuse.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-rs-02.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

**Device cluster.** C (imperative), G (plausible deniability).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-tm-02 self-convicting question

```text
handle: Three-Mountain Pass | axis: upward | imposed by: powerful men who cannot answer without exposing themselves
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker puts a question to the powerful that they cannot answer without convicting themselves.

**Surface.** A closing rhetorical question to a class of great men, in the terms of the innocent figure, naming their weakness.

**Fold.** The question traps its addressees: any answer admits the second meaning, and silence concedes it.

**Active site.** The question.

**Forbidden.** Naming a person. Plain accusation. An answer.

**Knowledge test.** The addressees cannot answer without revealing what they know.

**Stake.** High. It needs a real gap in power.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-tm-03, cf-rs-02. Cancels with cf-he-03, which forbids a question whose answer is implied.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation), A (addressee position).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-tm-03 praised carver

```text
handle: Three-Mountain Pass | axis: upward | imposed by: a body that can be admired only as a work of art or nature
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker can praise the body only by praising whoever made the landscape it is disguised as.

**Surface.** An early question of admiration for the maker of the scene, followed by the scene's features.

**Fold.** Praising the carver turns the body into a masterpiece. Admiration is allowed where desire is not.

**Active site.** The question of praise.

**Forbidden.** Naming the body. Praising the woman directly.

**Knowledge test.** No one can hold the praise to the body.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the disguise is necessary.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-tm-02, cf-tb-03. See Section D.

**Engines admitted.** Steganographic surface.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation), D (conceit).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-co-02 unbidden grief

```text
handle: Confession | axis: upward | imposed by: a sorrow that sounds without the speaker's act
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker's grief sounds on its own, without her having set it going.

**Surface.** Parallel statements that grief and sorrow are sounding though she did not sound them, figured as instruments that play themselves.

**Fold.** Grief that plays itself puts the cause outside her will. The reader sees her suffering as imposed.

**Active site.** The second of the parallel lines.

**Forbidden.** Her causing it. Her stopping it.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know why the grief sounds, or stop it.

**Stake.** Moderate. It holds where the grief is really not of her making.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-co-03, cf-me-02.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** B (parallelism), D (metaphor).

**Stand-down.** None.

### cf-co-03 disguise question

```text
handle: Confession | axis: upward | imposed by: a verdict that demands the speaker hide what she is
rights ceiling: in copyright (translation), Level 0, structure only
```

**Position.** The speaker asks whether she must disguise her nature to satisfy a verdict against it.

**Surface.** A closing question naming the demanded disguise, left unanswered.

**Fold.** Naming the disguise refuses it. The reader hears the question as defiance.

**Active site.** The question.

**Forbidden.** Adopting the disguise. An answer.

**Knowledge test.** She cannot know whether the disguise is required, since no one answers.

**Stake.** Moderate to high. It needs a real verdict against her.

**Assembly.** Combines with cf-co-02, cf-pw-02. Cancels with cf-he-03, which forbids a question whose answer is implied.

**Engines admitted.** Weak signal.

**Device cluster.** C (interrogation), E (closure).

**Stand-down.** None.

## C. Overlays

An overlay is not a confinement. It is an additional rule applicable to any confinement in Section A or B, and it has no position of its own.

A second class of overlay lives outside this file, from v0.13. A speaker dial setting from `13-speaker-dials` also adds a requirement and removes nothing knowable, so it is an overlay by this section's definition. It changes the carrier: the means by which the trial delivers what its confinement allows. The Provenance notes below have recorded carriers corpus by corpus without a field for them: jokes, anecdotes, and borrowed idiom in the stand-up corpus; decorum, prayer, and inherited figures in the 1900 collection; documents and found language in the recent books; grammar in the Beckett selection; double entendre in the Hồ Xuân Hương selection. A dial overlay asks whether a confinement's fold survives a change of carrier. It runs as a pair under Section D so that the seam can answer.

### ov-01 ars poetica

**Rule.** The practical activity in the poem must have the same shape as the poem's own compositional procedure, and the poem may never say so. Choose the activity for its rules. Never mention writing.

**Why it is an overlay.** It adds a requirement and removes nothing knowable. Nothing becomes unavailable to the speaker, so it fails the knowledge test and cannot be a confinement.

**Gate.** This is the cheapest available reading and no poem can refuse it, so it does not fire by default and is not selected by the reasoning path. It requires the command `+ ars poetica`. When applied, the trial must report which of the specimen's strangest local choices the reflexive reading makes necessary. If the answer is none, the overlay did nothing and the trial says so.

**Forbidden.** Naming writing, poems, language, the page, or the reader. Any activity chosen for its resemblance to composition rather than for its rules.

**Interaction.** Cancels with cf-sc-05, which requires a speaker who cannot see what he is doing. Runs on everything else. With cf-sb-01 and cf-sb-03 it runs in the functional rather than the figural mode: a destroyed vernacular object did the work the poem now does, and the succession stays unstated.

**Stake note.** The overlay has no stake of its own and does not repair a denatured confinement. Applying it to a costume trial produces a costume trial with a thesis.

## D. Combination and Cancellation

Combination is at primitive level only. Composites do not combine.

**Ceiling: one refusal per trial.** Refusal primitives are cf-sc-02, cf-sb-04, cf-wa-04, cf-pr-02, cf-me-03, and cf-eb-02. Two in one trial over-supply withholding, and when everything is withheld nothing reads as withheld.

**Cap: three primitives per trial**, plus at most one overlay. Beyond three the trial is executing a specification rather than running a confinement, and the seam note becomes unwritable because there are too many candidate causes.

**Dial overlays, from v0.13.** A speaker dial setting counts against the one-overlay cap, so ov-01 and a dial setting do not run in one trial. Two overlays give the seam two candidate causes, which is the reason for the primitive cap as well. A dial overlay runs as a pair: the first trial runs the confinement without it, the second runs the same confinement over the same span with it, and the second seam reports what changed at the fold. The pair is the two default trials.

| cf-hl-03 + cf-pr-03 | An act approached only as unperformed, once forward and once backward |

| cf-jc-03 + cf-sc-05 | An earnest speaker who cannot see his register gap and cannot read his own evidence. The unknowing speaker at full strength, and possibly more than one trial can carry |

**Recorded recurrence.** cf-ap-01 runs a variant of cf-wl-04 without having been built from it: the renunciation is contradicted in sleep rather than in syntax. A primitive recurring unprompted in an unrelated specimen is the first evidence in the file that a primitive is an operation rather than a style of its source. It is one case.

cf-pr-05 is a second case, weaker than the first. The frame it describes runs cf-wl-02's operation, a load-bearing text reported as written by another hand, and it was extracted as its own primitive because its fold turns on the premise being false in its source. The two cancel. A later sweep decides whether cf-pr-05 is a specialization of cf-wl-02.

**Recurrence across periods, v0.6.** Three of the five stand-up specimens run an earlier primitive in a variant without having been built from it. Assailant runs cf-et-02, retracted access: full narrated knowledge of a man's life, then the disclosure that her knowledge is a police description. Smithville runs cf-ap-04, unclassified state, on a scene's register rather than a sleep's kind. How to Like It closes on a domestic cf-mt-04, sole custodian. The file's primitives were extracted from poems written between the sixteenth century and 1946, and they turn up unprompted in poems from the 1980s and 1990s. The recurrences were recorded, not given new primitives. Batch 2 adds a fourth: Ultrasound runs cf-he-02, address to the unborn, made tender rather than prophetic, and cf-mt-03, instrument limit, on an imaging device. Four of the ten stand-up specimens run an earlier primitive in variant.

**Recurrence, v0.8.** Three of the five 1900 specimens run earlier primitives in variant. Proud Name runs cf-wa-03, split display, in a year of public laughter over private despair, and cf-me-04, borrowed testimony, since the man's death reaches the speaker only as others report it. Known Heaven runs cf-cl-04, self-convicting reason. Path of Dreams runs cf-ap-04, unclassified state, on the far shore, and repeats Apple-Picking's pairing of sleep with death. One move also recurs inside the book: Proud Name and Yonder Hill both close on a wish to have died in another's place. The primitives now recur across four centuries of specimens, in both directions from the periods they were drawn from.

**Recurrence, v0.9.** Four of the five second-pass specimens run earlier primitives in variant. Not Hear runs cf-cl-02, exhaustive foreclosure, closing each appeal the living could make before its verdict. Prisoned Within and Winding Sheet both run cf-mc-03, posthumous verdict, one as a witness who sees the self only after the body is gone and one as a token of love that reaches the speaker only in death. Love Passed By runs cf-jc-03, unread evidence, in a dropped flower. Across the two passes on this book, seven of ten specimens run an earlier primitive.

**Recurrence, v0.10.** All five specimens from the recent books run or invert an earlier primitive. Passed a Soul states its worst fact once and never returns to it, cf-sb-04, flat predicate, recurring in another poem by the Small Branch author, the first within-author recurrence in the file. Old Glory runs cf-sc-05, unknowing register, at the scale of a whole language. Amazon History runs cf-jc-02, remains as record. Shaking Hands runs cf-te-02, bodily knowledge. Punctum inverts cf-hi-02. Inside Vuong's book, Amazon History and Old Glory share the title as their only frame. The base rate has risen with the catalog: with ninety-nine primitives on file, a new specimen has more to match, so recurrence counts across batches are not directly comparable.

**Recurrence, v0.11.** Five of the nine Beckett specimens run primitives extracted from other poets, two from poems written decades later and three from poems written about a decade earlier: Enueg I runs Santos's work instead of grief, Alba runs Ransom's failed reception, Da Tagte Es runs Crane's instrument limit, Echo's Bones runs Crane's sole custodian, and Ooftish runs Vuong's custodial voice. Inside the selection, the truncated formula of Enueg II recurs in Sanies II. What these poems add is a carrier the file had seen only in pieces: grammar. The limit is held by a sentence that never reaches its main clause, a condition left without its consequence, a proverb cut off before its permission, a refrain whose permissions shrink, and a closing word that refuses.

**Recurrence, v0.12.** Five of the nine Hồ Xuân Hương specimens run primitives extracted from other poets, all of them written later: Offering Betel runs Beckett's null reading in the positive, Girl without a Sex runs Frost's unclassified state, Confession runs Frost's absent arbiter, and Two Beauties runs Trethewey's photographer outside the frame. Three-Mountain Pass runs River Snail's speakable figure within the same poet. The larger recurrence is structural. The catalog's sideways axis, withheld behind decorum in front of people who supply the missing term, and the kernel's Steganographic surface both describe her double entendre exactly, and the device inventory names her as its calibration case for the Nôm opt-in. Her poems are the operation the file's vocabulary was built around, arriving at its end.

**Recurrence, v0.16.** Jackfruit, held at v0.12 as a variant of River Snail, runs two of the same poet's primitives together: cf-rs-02, speakable figure, and cf-ob-03, fated test. It is the fated test's second case. Offering Betel sets love as a condition and names the sign that will show on the other party if the condition holds; Jackfruit does the same inside the figure, with the sign on the tester's hands rather than his mouth, and the figure carrying what Offering Betel carried plainly. Recorded as a recurrence and not given an entry, on the file's standing rule that a primitive recurring unprompted is evidence the primitive is an operation rather than a style of one poem; a within-poet recurrence is the weaker form of that evidence, as the v0.10 note on the Small Branch author records. The hold at v0.12 stands corrected in one respect: Jackfruit is a variant of River Snail in its figure and of Offering Betel in its test, and the family it was filed under has one member that runs a second primitive. cf-rs-01's Assembly line, which lists Jackfruit among the held variants, is left as written; this paragraph supersedes it for Jackfruit. The other held poems of the family, Weaving at Night, The Paper Fan, Swinging, and Picking Flowers, were not re-examined.

**Assembly from v0.3 on is partial.** Each primitive added from v0.3 on was checked within its specimen and against the ten seed primitives, and batch-2 primitives against batch 1 where a pairing was apparent. Pairs across the new specimens are recorded where found and were not run exhaustively. An unrecorded pair involving a v0.3 or v0.4 entry is untested, not neutral. Seed entries' assembly fields are not back-filled; this section carries the new pairs.

## E. Selection Rule

When a rewrite command names confinements, use those and do not substitute. When it selects on `axis`, `handle`, or `imposed by`, take the matching set and choose within it by the rule below. When it names nothing, run the rule on the whole catalog.

**Select against the specimen's own axis, not with it.** A draft withholding inward is offered upward, sideways, and outward. A draft running an outward limit is offered the three withholdings. Similarity produces a trial that tells the poet what the draft already does.

Default two trials, three maximum.

**Abstain when only costume is available.** If the draft's material is low-stakes and every candidate's stake field warns against it, say so and ship the extraction alone. Empty bins stay honest, and one trial that reaches something beats three that assemble surfaces. Abstention is a completed judgment.

**Do not select a primitive whose knowledge test the draft cannot satisfy.** A draft with a mobile speaker cannot take cf-sb-02 without becoming a different poem, which is an engine swap wearing a confinement's name.

## F. Rights and Leak Control

A confinement is a procedure, and a procedure is not protected expression, so this catalog is clean in principle. Three paths leak in practice.

**Entry illustration.** Entries describe operations and do not quote the operations performing themselves. Public-domain sources are available at Level 4 and are held at Level 0 or 1 here anyway, because an entry needing a line to be understood has not finished describing its operation. In-copyright sources are held at Level 0, structure only.

**Composite specificity.** A composite detailed enough to reconstruct its source is a copy in procedural clothing. Run the kernel's Reassembly Test on the composite entry itself, not only on quotations. The test question: could a competent writer, given this entry and the subject, produce something a reader would mistake for the source? If yes, cut detail until no.

**Trial output.** A trial reproduces operations, never phrasing. A trial that arrives at the source's own sentences has stopped running the confinement and started reproducing the poem.

**Life dates.** A poet's dates do not settle a rights ceiling. The Huswifery author died in 1729, and the poem was first published in 1939, so its ceiling is uncertain and the entry holds at Level 0. Where first publication was not checked, the entry says so and holds at Level 0.

**Specimens reprinted inside a calibration corpus.** Where a specimen's text appears in a loaded module's calibration corpus with the source's commentary on it, the specimen is left out of selection even when its author is not a calibration source, because an entry could restate the source's reading of the poem. `0c-voice-designations` covers author collisions and points here for this case, from its v0.7.

**Entries that predate a calibration corpus.** From v0.15. Where a specimen already in this catalog is reprinted and read in a calibration corpus adopted later, the rule above cannot run, since there is no selection to exclude it from, and the entries are not removed. They stand, because their reading was written before the source was in the bundle and any convergence with the source's reading is independent. Each such entry carries a Convergence field naming the extension, the catalog version at which the entry was extracted, and the freeze: the entry is not revised in any session where that extension is loaded, because a revision made with the source open could not be told from a restatement. No further entries are extracted from the specimen while the extension is in the bundle. The first case is Prufrock, extracted at v0.4 and read in the calibration source of `14-attridge-staten-extraction`.

**Attribution.** An entry's handle and stake depend on who wrote the specimen. A corpus whose attribution is in doubt is repaired before selection, and each specimen's attribution standing is recorded in Provenance.

`stand-down` records a jurisdiction limit rather than a rights limit. Where a specimen's author also calibrates a loaded voice or a loaded mode's own material, entries from that specimen stand down for that voice or mode, per `0c-voice-designations` v0.4, and the stand-down is recorded in output as a jurisdiction note without stated cause. This file does not name which entries those would be, following the precedent in `0-poetics-boot-hollow`, which records that a collision exists without disclosing the mapping.

Rejected as confinements, recorded so they are not proposed again:

- **Formal schemes with no knowledge test.** Sonnet, villanelle, sestina. These are constraints, they are elected, and nothing becomes unknowable. They belong in the Extended Device Inventory, Category E.
- **Procedural generators** where the procedure is chosen for novelty. Strangeness is cheap and a generator without an epistemic limit produces arbitrary output the apparatus would credit as a confinement. The necessity test in Section H applies.
- **Excerpts and sections as composite sources.** A composite is a whole-poem confinement. An excerpt carries the anthologist's cut as its limit, which is the Jubilate Agno problem. Excerpted items and numbered sections of longer sequences may yield primitives on the page and are not composite candidates.
- **Limits that belong to characters.** Five Lives: the observed creatures cannot know their world, and the observer knows all of it; leaving the human application unstated is a choice, not a limit. The March into Virginia: the speaker writes from the outcome, and the ignorance is the soldiers'. Dramatic irony in the speaker's favor passes no knowledge test.
- **Speakers who claim total access.** Thy Brother's Blood: the speaker reads hidden guilt by the Spirit, so nothing is unknowable to the poem.
- **Limits that belong to characters, stand-up corpus.** The History Teacher (Collins): the teacher withholds history from his students, and the poem knows everything he hides.
- **Nothing unknowable, stand-up corpus.** Lawrence (Hoagland): a resolve to speak next time, with nothing withheld. Working at the Wholesale Curtain Showroom (Ochester): an anecdote of praise. The World in My Mother's Hair (Glatt): the limit belongs to the mother, a character the speaker sees through.
- **Nothing unknowable, 1900 collection.** The Shylock portrait: the speaker claims to see the demon behind the mask, and the portrait is built on an antisemitic stereotype. The Seville monologue ending in murder. The poet's two songs: the limit belongs to the song's audience, and the poem sees exactly what the song hides. It restates the kernel's Register Is Not Referent as a fable, which is the only reason it is recorded.
- **Nothing unknowable, Hồ Xuân Hương selection.** On Sharing a Husband, a plain complaint that says what it knows. Male Member, whose title in this file names the thing the figure would otherwise hide. Autumn Landscape and Tavern by a Mountain Stream, landscapes with nothing withheld.
- **Nothing unknowable, Beckett selection.** Antipepsis: a satire in couplets on a city appalled that a thought has occurred, with no limit on what the poem knows.
- **Forms, recent books.** Saying No and Pentatina for Five Vowels (McGrath): a comic series of refusals turning to assent, and a vowel-rhyme scheme. Both elected.
- **Forms.** All-Purpose Apology Poem (Straus): a template apology with blanks, elected rather than imposed. Route to Form Examination.
- **Accepted terms with nothing unknowable.** Terminus: a god states the terms of age and the speaker obeys them. A constraint, by the formal-schemes rule.

## H. Failure Modes

### Denatured Transfer

Position and surface transfer, the fold collapses, and the trial produces a competent costume. The characteristic failure of the whole catalog.

Correction: read the `stake` field before selecting, not after. If the draft's material cannot bear the original's cost, either select a different primitive or abstain. If the trial has already been written, report it in the seam line rather than shipping the trial as a success.

### Confinement as Engine

A whole-poem drive is entered or selected as a confinement. The two are on different scales and a drive has no knowledge test.

Correction: run the knowledge test. If nothing becomes unknowable, it is an engine and belongs in Engine Bay, or a device and belongs in the inventory.

### Fold Restated as Surface

The fold field paraphrases the position or repeats the surface rules, and the entry ships with three fields and two findings. The likeliest failure in routine extraction, because the two flat levels are easy to see and the fold is not.

Correction: run the fold prompt. If the answer can be assembled from the two fields above it, the fold has not been found. Hold the entry incomplete rather than filling it. A missing fold is recoverable and a wrong one is not, since a wrong fold will be selected against and will produce trials that reach nothing.

### Template Transfer

An entry carries the source's phrasing rather than its operation, and trials reproduce sentences.

Correction: rewrite the field as an operation. Cut every clause that could be pasted.

### Trial Capture

A trial is read as a revision route and the poet is handed the apparatus's poem.

Correction: the firewall in the workshop module. A trial enters Revision Routes only when the draft's own visible material supports the move independently, in which case the route is stated from the draft.

### Over-Supplied Refusal

Two or more refusals in one trial, so nothing reads as withheld.

Correction: the Section D ceiling. One refusal per trial.

### Confinement Shopping

Three trials ship because the form has three slots.

Correction: default two, abstain when the stake fields warn. Forced fill fails Checkpoint 6.

### Credited Strangeness

An arbitrary procedure is entered because its output is odd. Surrealist automatic writing is maximally strange and mostly unreadable.

Correction: the necessity test. A confinement earns its entry when the detour it forces reaches something the straight path cannot, and when the result reads as necessary in retrospect while not having been available in advance. Strangeness that only gates attention is the cult-mimicry failure. Test the confinement, do not credit it.

### Model Attribution

The four structural levels, the active site, or the denaturation reading are attributed to the poet.

Correction: Period Fit. The levels may be real on the page. The model is the reader's.

## Provenance

Occasioned by an operator instruction in September 2026, following a sequence of live tests in which three confinements were extracted from read specimens and applied to an unrelated subject. All three transferred at position and surface and all three failed at the same seam, which is where the `stake` field and the denaturation reading come from. The structural model is the operator's protein analogy, taken up because it separates the level that transfers from the level that does not.

The composite-and-primitive split answers a problem the tests produced directly: composites do not combine, because the second refusal cancels the first.

v0.2 expands the fold level after the operator observed that the operative definition was three sentences in the file that gets loaded, while the fuller treatment sat in `0d-apparatus-provenance`, which nothing loads. The history stays there. The procedure is here.

v0.1 seeds three specimens, two public domain and one in copyright at Level 0. The kernel carries the compatibility contract at v0.3.14. `3a-workshop` v0.5.0 carries the Confinement Bay that consumes this file. `0-poetics-boot-hollow` carries the registry entry, without which a future module could be built onto identifier 12.

v0.3 extracts batch 1 of 2 from an operator-supplied corpus, a cleaned OCR text of an anthology printing American poems from Edward Taylor to Robert Lowell. Its contents match Selden Rodman's One Hundred American Poems (1948); that identification is the apparatus's (P4). The cleaning pass had overwritten the true author on roughly a third of the records, so attribution was repaired before selection. Batch 1 was selected against the knowledge test, with axis coverage as the tiebreak: one upward, two sideways, two inward.

| Handle | Poem | Author | Attribution standing | Text notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Huswifery | Huswifery | Edward Taylor | header agrees with the text | intact |
| Eros Turannos | Eros Turannos | Edwin Arlington Robinson | header agrees with the text | one dropped apostrophe in the fifth stanza; no bearing on the extraction |
| Apple-Picking | After Apple-Picking | Robert Frost | true header on the page; the cleaned stamp named another poet | OCR debris after the last line |
| Hellenistics | Hellenistics | Robinson Jeffers | true header on the page with the name OCR-damaged, repaired by recognition (P4); the cleaned stamp named another poet | the first movement ends mid-clause at a page boundary, so text is probably lost there (P4); the active site is intact |
| Wife's Affection | Rearrange a Wife's Affection | Emily Dickinson | header agrees with the text | normalized text printed as one block; punctuation-dependent surface not read |

Two findings from batch 1 go past the entries. The Eros Turannos fold holds on a cost the page carries, the town's, whether or not its biographical referent is real, which is what the stake convention generalizes. And the axis field strains on blindness: Apple-Picking and Hellenistics are limits the speaker cannot see past rather than withholdings anyone performs, and both are filed inward because selection needs one of the three values. The strain is recorded, not fixed.

v0.4 extracts batch 2 of 2 from the same corpus, after operator review of batch 1. Selection ran against the knowledge test with axis coverage as the tiebreak, two upward, two sideways, one inward, which brings the file to four upward, five sideways, and four inward composites. Batch 2 repeats two batch-1 authors, Dickinson and Robinson, which narrows the specimen range. No batch-2 specimen is in copyright.

| Handle | Poem | Author | Attribution standing | Text notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cannot Live | I Cannot Live With You | Emily Dickinson | header agrees with the text | the 1890 editors' text, printed as one block; two word-level OCR errors and a bracket for a final dash, none at the active site |
| Many Are Called | Many Are Called | Edwin Arlington Robinson | header agrees with the text | the last line's adjective is lost to OCR, at the active site; structure intact |
| Prufrock | The Love Song of J. Alfred Prufrock | T. S. Eliot | true header on the page; the cleaned stamp named another poet | epigraph present with apostrophes lost; body intact |
| Philomela | Philomela | John Crowe Ransom | true header on the page; the cleaned stamp named another poet | intact apart from stray punctuation |
| Melville's Tomb | At Melville's Tomb | Hart Crane | header agrees with the text | intact |

Many Are Called shares Huswifery's position, a standing the elected cannot know, and differs from it in every operation. That is some evidence that position alone does not fix a confinement. Melville's Tomb is the third blindness case filed inward, after Apple-Picking and Hellenistics, so the axis strain recorded at v0.3 now has three instances.

v0.5 applies four operator calls. The seed entries' stake fields are regraded to the stake convention. The Snow Crust woman's existence stays at P4, since the page asserts her and nothing on it or in its paratext shows her. The axis set gains outward, and the three blindness cases move to it. That leaves inward with one specimen, Small Branch, so inward is now the thinnest axis in the file: four upward, six sideways, one inward, three outward. The Jewish Cemetery at Newport is taken as a second unknowing-register case, and the stand-down now reaches modes, per `0c-voice-designations` v0.4.

The publisher's contributor list names Bukowski and Clifton, and a reader review names the opening poems in the file's order (P2 for both records; the match is the apparatus's). Attribution was repaired by script before selection: fourteen fused title-and-byline lines split, which returned six poems to their authors, nine OCR name variants fixed, and one misplaced boundary moved. The cleaner also joined whole sentences onto single lines in about ninety poems, which cannot be undone from the file, so no v0.6 entry reads line breaks. Every specimen is in copyright and held at Level 0. None of the five authors is a calibration source. Selection ran against the knowledge test from the held pool and a further reading of six poems, weighted toward outward and upward. None of the five authors is a calibration source. The file now stands at six upward, eight sideways, four inward, and six outward. The stand-up corpus yielded ten confinements, the ceiling, where the expectation had been fewer. Its poems are plain-spoken and comic, and the register did not keep them from holding real knowledge limits. What it changed is how the limits are carried. By the apparatus's reading, seven of the ten carry the limit through a joke, an anecdote, or a borrowed idiom, and none carries it through difficulty.


v0.8 extracts from a third operator-supplied corpus: a file of JSON lines, one line of verse per record, with a text field and a gid field reading 27024, which reads as a Project Gutenberg ebook number (P4). The operator dates the book 1900 (P2 as the operator's statement), and its first line and one sonnet carry the title In Fortune's Twilight. Everything in it is in the public domain; entries hold at Level 0 as elsewhere.

The file names no author, and four searches did not identify one. Two pieces of internal evidence are on the page (P0): a sonnet addressed to R. D. MacLean as a player-king, and a monologue spoken by an actress playing Rosalind. MacLean was a Shakespearean actor who married the actress Odette Tyler in 1897, not long after his first wife, Marie Prescott, died (P2, per Wikipedia's articles on both). That Tyler wrote this book is the apparatus's inference (P4). So is the resemblance between Prescott's death and the situation of Yonder Hill, which Register Is Not Referent keeps a hypothesis. Attribution stands as unidentified until the operator supplies it.

The file carries no poem titles beyond the book's, no stanza breaks, and no boundaries between poems, so every boundary used here is the apparatus's inference (P4). Italics survive as underscores. Several poems appear to lack a line, among them the Seville monologue and the Rosalind monologue, which suggests the source dropped lines. Of the five specimens, only the sequence shows gaps.

The file now stands at seven upward, nine sideways, six inward, and seven outward. Against the stand-up corpus, the 1900 book carries its limits through decorum, prayer, and inherited figures rather than through jokes, and it yielded five confinements by the knowledge test, with five more held.

v0.9 makes a second pass on the 1900 collection at the operator's request: at most five more, with the rejected poems skipped. Three came from the v0.8 held list. The Rosalind monologue stays held, because its text appears truncated and a composite needs a whole poem, and the forbidden-shore poem stays held on a thin knowledge test. The other two places went to poems not read closely at v0.8. Attribution stands as unidentified, as at v0.8.

v0.10 extracts from three recent single-author books supplied as PDFs: Sherod Santos, Square Inch Hours (W. W. Norton, 2017); Campbell McGrath, Nouns & Verbs: New and Selected Poems (2019); and Ocean Vuong, Time Is a Mother (Penguin Press, 2022), each dated by its copyright page (P0). All three are in copyright and held at Level 0, and nothing from them is reproduced here. Every page of all three files carries the watermark of OceanofPDF.com (P0). The catalog records the files' provenance and holds structure only.

None of the three authors calibrates a voice or a mode. Santos is the private calibration source for six kernel directives, and his Where Once a Small Branch Fell, in this book, is the Small Branch seed. The kernel's 0.3.12 note treats directive calibration as specimen provenance rather than voice calibration, so the designation layer's stand-down does not fire; the seed precedent already takes Santos as a specimen. ASSAY's corpus holds a 2001 interview of Santos by its calibration source, which predates this book and discusses none of its poems. The McGrath who appears in JIG's corpus is Thomas McGrath. No text from any of the three books appears in any calibration corpus.

The file now stands at nine upward, eleven sideways, nine inward, and ten outward. The three books carry their limits differently. Vuong's carry them in documents and found language: a purchase record, a stack of idioms, a retouched photograph with its custodians' voice. That fits the guess, recorded in conversation before these books arrived, that the document would be the carrier now. Santos's carry them in withheld feeling and in the body, in prose. McGrath's sample yielded none: its mode is expansive and public, and it says what it knows.

v0.12 extracts from an operator-supplied selection titled Ho Xuan Huong Selected Translations: twenty-three poems in one Markdown file, one of them the reply of another poet. The translations are John Balaban's, from Spring Essence: The Poetry of Hồ Xuân Hương (Copper Canyon Press, 2000): the publisher's and reviewers' pages name him as translator, and the lines they quote match the file (P2). The originals were written in Nôm around 1800, and the poet's life, the scholarly view that she was a concubine, and the attribution of individual poems are all uncertain or debated (P4, apart from one review's report, P2). The translations are in copyright and held at Level 0. Two proper names in the file carry OCR damage to their diacritics.

Every entry reads the translation. The originals' Tang-regulated forms and Nôm wordplay are recorded as P4 and read from nothing on the page. The device inventory makes Nôm devices an explicit opt-in and names this poet as their calibration case (P0); the opt-in was not invoked, and an English file could not carry script-level devices if it were. Hồ Xuân Hương calibrates no voice or mode, and her calibration role for the opt-in is specimen provenance, as with Santos. No text from the file appears in any module. The translator is also a poet in the stand-up corpus (P0).

v0.13 extracts nothing. It admits speaker dial settings from `13-speaker-dials` as a second class of overlay, after the operator supplied a craft book on how poems sound and asked whether its controls could run in the workshop's trials. The answer turned on this file's knowledge test: every control in the book changes how a poem says what it knows and none changes what it can know, so none is a confinement and all are overlay-class. The book's own poetics runs toward disclosure, which is why this file had already rejected one of its author's poems as having nothing withheld. What the dial file adds here is a name for the carrier these notes have been recording since v0.7, and a pair form that lets a trial report whether a fold survives a change of it. The entry counts are unchanged.
